ArtsMusicNAC preview : Matt Mays , at 40 , ready to get ' weirder'http : **31;28810;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;28843;TOOLONG ... title= " Matt Mays. " alt= " Matt Mays. " data-portal-copyright= " Devin-McLean " **28;28879;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1491271 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;NAC Presents Matt Mays : Howl at the Night tour&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;with Skye Wallace&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;8:30 p.m . Nov. 3 , Babs Asper Theatre , National Arts Centre&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian rocker Matt Mays is making his way across the country on the first theatre tour of his two-decade-plus career . Dubbed Howl at the Night , the journey brings him and a full band to 18 cities , ending with two shows in hometown Halifax , and including his first appearance at the National Arts Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In this edited interview , the **27;28909;TOOLONG talks about the tour , how his new single , Let There Be Love , came out of his struggle with anxiety , and the liberating effect of turning 40. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : It 's often a challenge for artists to make the jump from playing clubs to theatres . What 's your secret ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : I think it 's just because I 've been around so long . This is my 20th fall tour . The first tour was 1999 , touring across Canada with the Guthries . I 've got a really strong relationship with people who come to the shows , and it 's nice they 're still coming out after all these years . We 've had some really special times in all these towns we 've been playing in for 20 years now . It 's great that people have stayed with me for so long , and they still come out ... especially Ottawa . It was our first town that really started showing up after Halifax . I 'm thankful for that . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Do you get nervous when everyone is sitting there quietly ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , the silence is weird . The whole pin drop thing , it definitely freaks me out but it also gives a chance for people to listen to some of the lyrics I 've worked really hard on . When you 're in a club , people are usually a bit hosed , or talking and it 's so loud . Do n't get me wrong , I do n't want people to think it 's going to be a super mellow quiet show because we plan to go berserk towards the end as usual . It 's going to be loud and crazy and you might not hear the lyrics . But it 's nice that the songs that I want to come across , it 's a lot easier for me to do that when it 's quieter and people ca n't move to get up . I 've kind of got them there . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Are you bringing a band ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , it 's the full eight-piece band . I 'm playing the first song solo , and we 're building the show , adding a musician for each of the first eight songs . Then the eight of us are going to play the rest of it together , just to crescendo the show . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Wow , that 's a big band . Why so many players ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : There 's a lot of parts in my tunes , and a lot of bands these days use backing tracks , just hit play on something . But I do n't want to do that . We 're all super close and we 've all toured a lot together this year , and it 's something I 've always wanted to do where no part is left out , whether it 's some percussion or background vocals . It just feels right . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;28938;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;28974;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You just released a cool new song , Let There Be Love . What inspired it ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : A few things , including friends of mine who are amazingly gifted and doing really well in their lives on the outside , but struggling and having a hard time figuring out life , as am I , and a lot of people around me . As I travel , I find I just sort of start leaning on things that are actually real , like friendship and love , and especially self-love , which is a tough one . Like a lot of people , I suffer from some pretty heavy-duty anxiety , which is , I know , ironic because I 'm the singer in a band . But I deal with that a lot and I 'm getting help for it . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : There are actually two versions of the song , one electric and one acoustic , like your last album , Once Upon a Hell of a Time , and its acoustic counterpart . What 's the appeal of doubling your workload ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : I think about music a lot , and I 've always been confused why a song has to end up one way and that has to be it for everybody . I think the process of writing it and putting it down in time , that has to be a real relationship , one on one , but after that , it can be done a million different ways , if it 's done from the heart . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Why release the song now ? Why not wait and put it on an album ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : I felt it was a good time , I did n't want to wait . I know there 's a zillion love songs out there , but this one , to me , has its own kind of niche because it 's about all the different kinds of love , not just love between two people . It 's about the omnipresent love that we should keep nurturing and keep the pilot light going on . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Sounds like a reminder that people could use right now . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , we have to remind ourselves to love ourselves and other people instead of our phones . I deleted any account that made me feel jealous or envious or anxious in general , and it changed my life . I did n't even know I had anxiety until I was 35 . I worry about young people who ca n't even identify it yet . Call it anxiety , nervousness , fear , whatever you want to call it , it 's a definite feeling and it 's OK to have it . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Is an album in the works , too ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , we 'll be getting in the studio this winter and get a record finished . I 've got a bunch of songs that I want to get out . I 've been working 9-5 in two studios in Toronto so I 've got a lot of material and the band is primed . I feel super liberated as an artist these days . I 'm just going to release a bunch of music and get weird , and go for it . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You turned 40 this year . Is it possible you 've grown up ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : ( Laughs ) Yeah , something kind of solidifies . To me , a lot of my heroes really mellowed out after 40 but I just want to get weirder , take more chances in music . I think maybe it 's just the times or whatever , but I feel my eccentricities are starting to show themselves more . There 's no dampers on any of it . I 'm not afraid to say no or yes . It 's really a nice feeling , discovering myself a little deeper . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; lsaxberg@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE DOWN TO BUSINESS PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;29010;TOOLONG ... Lynn SaxbergFri , 25 Oct 2019 11:11:52 **45;29052;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicThe Lazys jump into rock stardomhttp : **33;29099;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29134;TOOLONG ... title= " The Lazys " alt= " The Lazys " data-portal-copyright= " Warner Music Canada " **28;29170;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1485145 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Australian hard-rock band The Lazys have found a second home in Canada , thanks in part to the support of Billy Talent&amp;rsquo ; s Ian D&amp;rsquo ; Sa . He has produced , championed and otherwise supported the band since they first stepped foot on North American soil . As singer Leon Harrison tells Lynn Saxberg in this edited interview , the boost is also helping them crack the international market . Here&amp;rsquo ; s more from the conversation : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How does a band from Sydney , Australia become one of the hottest new acts in Canada ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : It goes back to 2014-15 , we did a lot of stuff at Canadian Music Week , met a bunch of people , including the one and only Ian D'Sa from Billy Talent . He saw us performing and started up a friendship pretty much directly after that . A few things happened , and we did a couple of songs with him that gave us our start at radio . From there , he was really interested to produce a full-length album , which we did in 2017 . It came out last year , and basically from there , it went to Europe and the U.K. , and Canada continued to support us . It all morphed from a music conference in Toronto . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What do you think makes Canada a good jumping off point for the global music industry ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : There 's a strong music industry in Toronto , really supportive . I guess Ian 's name and expertise was a major draw card , and producing with him was really good . It was a step up for us . And yeah , there 's a lot of rock ' n ' roll radio stations that are very supportive in Canada . It just started clicking . A lot of Australian bands have to move away to get any kind of recognition , and we 're one of them now . They 're more into that indie pop dance thing and we just did n't fit in so we left . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Is Canada a better market for rock ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Everybody loves a good rock band , and now we 're starting to experience that in the Europe and U.K. markets . I think if you write good songs and entertain people in a good old rock ' n roll fashion , they seem to respond . There are lot of rock bands in Canada , and a lot are starting to break through , and they 're hungry and I 'm happy to be part of that . We 're always battling the immortals , the Rolling Stones , AC/DC , Zeppelin , and all the grandpa bands but it boils down to the fact that in their day , they wrote great songs , had great soloing , and put on great shows , so there is a level of expectation if you 're a rock band . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Tell me about Tropical Hazards , the record that Ian D'Sa produced . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : I guess just a lot more time was put into the songwriting . Ian definitely helped . It was basically like a trio , me , him and Matt in the studio and just work until everybody 's happy . If everybody in the room is happy , others who listen will be . We do n't stop until we feel it 's worthy to release . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Amid all the testosterone-driven rock , there 's a sensitive ballad about murdered and missing Indigenous women called Daughter . Where did that come from ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : That kinda stems back to when I went to college . Before I dropped out to tour , I was doing Indigenous studies as part of my degree . I found it interesting because Australia has some of the same issues as Canada , and there 's a big gap between Indigenous and non-Indigenous . I heard about the epidemic in the western part of Canada , where women were going missing , and police were n't trying to find them or figure out what happened . So when you think about , it 's a pretty badass rock n roll song because we 're saying bugger you , we know what 's happening . People are n't stupid . If we get to use the record to voice concerns and issues that happen in society , that 's a good platform . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : The a new video that looks like a highlight reel from your travels over the past year . What kind of year has it been ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : It 's been crazy . That video kind of sums up the last 8-12 months . We 've been across Europe and the U.K. twice this year now . We did our first run of summer festivals through Europe and the U.K. , and played to the biggest crowds we 've ever played to . It was just so good for the band to play to all sorts of different audiences in different countries and meet people and hopefully they join the Lazys thing . They seem to really like what we do live and it translates and they have a good time . Again , that 's all you can do as a rock band . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : The song , Half Mast Blues , has a bit of a message to it , too . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , it 's pretty funny . It 's one we wanted to attack kinda like The Jack , the AC/DC song where you have a funny topic but you do n't really spell it out to people too obviously . So we made it a pirate and the fact that he continues to get a little drunk with his partner , and has a bit of erectile dysfunction disorder . It was just a bit of tongue in cheek . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What 's your approach to the live show ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Basically just leaving it all on the stage . We 're there to do a job and it 's really fun , and I guess you could relate it to a sporting event . You go out and get a goal , and make the fans happy and take the title and that 's what we do when we play live , and so far , so good . I think when you put your entire self into a performance , and it 's honest and real and engaging , then you ca n't really go wrong . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Is the buzz on the Lazys getting back to Australia now ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : To our demographic , yes . But there 's a lot of lazy people in the music industry there , and the rock radio system . Excuse the pun . They still play Creed and Live and all those bands nobody gives a shit about anymore . The emphasis is n't on bringing new talent . It 's a small place . There 's a lot of venues that shoot people down , neighbours that complain , gaming machines that take priority . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : I 'm curious to know if you had any connections in Canada before you decided to come to music week . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Not really . We just heard about this music conference , with bands getting opportunities from it , and nothing was really happening in Australia . We 'd done similar events in Australia and just been overlooked so it was kind of our calling card . But also at the time , it was a make or break situation as to whether we continued . Then we met Ian and he fell in love with the band , and we picked up a great agent and record label and it all started falling into place . The workload did n't get any easier but we stepped into the professional landscape . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Lazys&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;with Inire and Taming Sari&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **33;29200;TOOLONG : Wednesday , Oct. 16 , 8 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Brass Monkey , 250A Greenbank Rd. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tickets &amp; info&lt;/strong&gt; : &lt;a href= " http : //eventbrite.com " &gt;eventbrite.co ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN ARTS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29235;TOOLONG ... bets , Oct. 17 to 23 : Tegan and Sara , and a Korean film fest&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29270;TOOLONG ... new at the National Gallery : Six decades of photography in Japan , and Canada 's New Generation award winners&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29305;TOOLONG ... Andrew King to Amal El-Mohtar , your Ottawa fall reading guide&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Lynn SaxbergTue , 15 Oct 2019 20:03:05 **45;29340;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicNAC preview -- Sarah McLachlan : ' I want to suck the marrow out of every day'http : **36;29387;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29425;TOOLONG ... title= " Sarah McLachlan . " alt= " Sarah McLachlan . " data-portal-copyright= " Kharen Hill " **28;29461;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1475865 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;NAC Gala&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Sarah McLachlan&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Also performing : National Ballet of Canada principle dancer Greta Hodgkinson , singer-songwriter isk ? , actor Mani Soleymanlou and conductor Alexander Shelley with the NAC Orchestra&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;When and where : 6:30 p.m . Saturday , Southam Hall , National Arts Centre&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Tickets : Limited seats remain , priced around $200 , available at NAC box office and ticketmaster.ca . Includes pre-concert reception at 5:30 p.m. and post-concert dance . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; Superstar songstress Sarah McLachlan is taking a break from her mom duties and music-school responsibilities to come to Ottawa to star in the National Arts Centre&amp;rsquo ; s golden-anniversary gala on Saturday , an annual fundraiser for the NAC&amp;rsquo ; s Youth and Education Trust . The Canadian Music Hall of Famer is bringing her band , which includes Whitehorse members Luke Doucet and Melissa McClelland , and will also perform with the NAC Orchestra . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lynn Saxberg caught up with the 51-year-old musician to talk about playing with symphonies , the rewards and challenges of running a music school , and how being happy makes it tough to write introspective songs . Here&amp;rsquo ; s an edited version of the interview . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You are no stranger to symphony shows ; in fact , you did some over the summer . Do you enjoy presenting your music with the ultimate wall of sound behind you ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Yeah , it&amp;rsquo ; s such a fun way to present my music , to have that immense beauty and power behind you when you&amp;rsquo ; re performing . It feels pretty spectacular . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : The National Arts Centre has been commissioning charts so that emerging artists can play with symphonies . How many of your songs have charts ? What do you think of that idea ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Probably about 25 of my songs are charted , including about eight Christmas songs . It&amp;rsquo ; s a great hybrid . If theatre and symphonies want to continue to live , they need to be able to engage different audiences . I think the idea of stepping away from purely classical music , and delving into the pop world , or different genres , to get new audiences in and become aware of the beauty and amazingness of it , is great . We need to figure out new , innovative ways to keep bringing people in and growing as artists . It&amp;rsquo ; s all part of the big picture . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;29491;TOOLONG ... title= " Sarah McLachlan . " alt= " Sarah McLachlan . " data-portal-copyright= " Kharen Hill " **28;29527;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1475865 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Do you have formal training in your background ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Yeah , I spent 12 years studying classical guitar , six years of piano and eight years of theory . I went to the Maritime Conservatory of Music . It was the only thing available . I stuck with it until I was 17. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Now you run the Sarah McLachlan School of Music , which provides free musical training to at-risk children and youths . How is that going ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : It&amp;rsquo ; s amazing , thank you . We have three schools now . Vancouver is the mothership , and Surrey and Edmonton are smaller &amp;mdash ; they&amp;rsquo ; re after-school programs . We&amp;rsquo ; re in our 18th year and we serve over 1,000 kids over all our programs . It&amp;rsquo ; s an amazing thing to be part of , to be able to create a culture and space for kids to be able to experience what it is to play music , not only by yourself but together with other people . There&amp;rsquo ; s persistence and resilience in working hard at something and having that sense of achievement , and also going out in front of your peers and performing a song that you&amp;rsquo ; ve written yourself . It creates empathy , understanding and inclusivity and it&amp;rsquo ; s a beautiful thing to be part of . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You must have some success stories after 18 years . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Absolutely . We have lots of successes that are musically driven but we also have sometimes more powerful stories , like when a kid is transitioning , has no friends and hasn&amp;rsquo ; t really been able to speak about it . Then three years later , they&amp;rsquo ; re graduating , standing up in front of a room singing a song that they wrote about their experience , and saying , &amp;lsquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s no way I would have survived if I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have this place , this support , this second family of kids and teachers who believed in me and let me be me. &amp;rsquo ; I get goosebumps . I&amp;rsquo ; ve got so many stories like that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Do you have any plans to expand the school to other cities ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Right now in Edmonton and Surrey , we&amp;rsquo ; re still in regular schools . It&amp;rsquo ; s amazing to have that space but it&amp;rsquo ; s a little more limited . So we&amp;rsquo ; re looking for permanent space in both of those locations . Another thing we&amp;rsquo ; re really trying to do is build a different kind of fundraising model because up to now , I&amp;rsquo ; ve been doing most of it and it&amp;rsquo ; s just not sustainable . There&amp;rsquo ; s only one of me and there are three schools . But , for the first time , we did a fundraiser this year where we netted enough to cover our annual operating budget a year ahead . Which is huge . We&amp;rsquo ; ve never been in that position before . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What about your music career ? Are you working on anything these days ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Yes . I&amp;rsquo ; m slowly chipping away at writing for a new record . I don&amp;rsquo ; t feel any burning need to finish it . I always have a bit of a block when it comes to making a record because I know when you make a record , it means leaving . It means going on tour , going to promote it , and having two teenagers at home , one doesn&amp;rsquo ; t want to leave for very long . You need to be around if s&amp;ndash ; t hits the fan . Things can go south pretty quick . I just feel like I want to be home . I&amp;rsquo ; m really happy right now , too . So that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t really help . Maybe next year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Being happy is a problem ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Unfortunately and fortunately , when I&amp;rsquo ; m in trauma or something terrible is happening , or I&amp;rsquo ; m sad or upset , there are always lots of opportunities to mine that and write about it , and figure out a way through it . But there&amp;rsquo ; s not a lot of that going on right now . Nobody&amp;rsquo ; s dying , I&amp;rsquo ; m healthy , the kids are healthy . Life is absolutely fantastic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You do n't mind being in your 50s ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Other than my body pissing me off because it doesn&amp;rsquo ; t work as well as it used to , it&amp;rsquo ; s pretty amazing . I love psychologically where I&amp;rsquo ; m at . Things that used to bother me just don&amp;rsquo ; t matter now , and I&amp;rsquo ; m way better at looking at what&amp;rsquo ; s important , and how to spend my time . I feel incredibly lucky . I want to suck the marrow out of every day , especially the days that we are feeling good and healthy and strong . I just want to make the most of it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Last question : There 's been some reflection on the impact of Lilith Fair recently . Would you consider reviving it ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : People are always asking me to bring it back but again , it&amp;rsquo ; s bandwidth . I was 27 years old and didn&amp;rsquo ; t have any kids , and had way more time on my hands . I think it would be great for a lot of reasons to bring it back but I honestly think it would take someone of a younger generation to spearhead it . I feel like if Taylor Swift or Lizzo wanted to take on something like this and be all about women&amp;rsquo ; s empowerment and equality and inclusiveness , then hell yeah . I could get on board with that . I just don&amp;rsquo ; t know if I could spearhead it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; lsaxberg@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29557;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicRIP , jazz piano great Harold Mabernhttp : **34;29709;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;29745;TOOLONG ... title= " Jazz pianist Harold Mabern plays Salt Restaurant on Preston Street Aug. 15 with Toronto saxophonist Kirk MacDonald " alt= " Jazz pianist Harold Mabern plays Salt Restaurant on Preston Street Aug. 15 with Toronto saxophonist Kirk MacDonald " data-portal-copyright= " Alan Nahigian " **28;29781;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 593651 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Harold Mabern , a Memphis-raised pianist beloved for his soulfulness and power at the keyboard and his gift of gab off the bandstand , has died , his record label Smoke Sessions Records announced Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mabern , who continued to tour , record and play in New York&amp;rsquo ; s jazz clubs with players half his age , was 83 . WBGO&amp;rsquo ; s Nate Chinen reports &lt;a href= " https : **33;29811;TOOLONG ... that Mabern died of a heart attack , and that he is survived by two children and a granddaughter , while his wife , Beatrice , died in 2010. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Word of Mabern&amp;rsquo ; s passing prompted tributes throughout the online jazz community . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29846;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29881;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29916;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;29951;TOOLONG ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Guitarist Peter Bernstein wrote on Facebook : &amp;ldquo ; Mr. Mabes I am having trouble finding words to say goodbye to you . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to imagine the world without you . A true master of music but also of giving , you are generosity personified . All we can do is remember you and try to be better people . R.I.P . Maestro Mabern. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;div&gt;Born on March 20 , 1936 , the musically self-taught Mabern grew up under the influence of the stellar pianist Phineas Newborn Jr . and was soon running with such peers as Frank Strozier , Booker Little and George Coleman . Mabern and Coleman were friends throughout the pianist 's lifetime , and Mabern plays on Coleman 's upcoming album , The Quartet . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;&lt;br&gt; Mabern moved to Chicago in 1954 , where he played with such elite saxophonists as Johnny Griffin , Gene Ammons and Clifford Jordan . Five years later , he moved to New York , where he would join the groups of Lionel Hampton and Art Farmer and Benny Golson 's Jazztet , Miles Davis and Wes Montgomery . **35;29986;TOOLONG ... &lt;div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 480 " src= " https : **34;30023;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;30059;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;&lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt;Mabern released more than 20 albums as a leader . A favourite of mine is the trio album Straight Street , a Japanese recording that also features bassist Ron Carter and drummer Jack DeJohnette . Mabern appears on almost 100 albums as a sideman. &lt;/div&gt; &lt;div&gt; Mabern taught for more than three decades at New Jersey 's William Paterson University , where his students included drummers Joe Farnsworth and saxophonist Eric Alexander , two younger musicians who would join him on innumerable performances after they graduated . &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Until his passing , Mabern was the quintessential New York jazzman working in the trenches , playing past midnight at Smalls Jazz Club or Smoke , two bastions of straight-ahead , swinging music . &lt;br&gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30095;TOOLONG ... title= " Harold Mabern , hitting hard at Smalls in September 2017 " alt= " Harold Mabern , hitting hard at Smalls in September 2017 " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;30131;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1243172 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;In recent years&lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt; , Mabern developed more connections with Canada 's jazz scene . He played and recorded with saxophonists Kirk MacDonald and Cory Weeds , and is featured on two of MacDonald 's most recent albums , including the Juno-winning Vista Obscura . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here are clips of Mabern with MacDonald ( playing in Montreal ) and Weeds ( playing in Vancouver ) , throwing himself into some minor blues with his driving , swirling approach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;30161;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;30197;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;30233;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;30269;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; On Facebook , MacDonald wrote : &amp;ldquo ; I was shocked and deeply saddened to hear the devastating news of Harold Mabern&amp;rsquo ; s passing . We have lost a musical giant ! Harold&amp;rsquo ; s spirit and unconditional generosity with his gifts and wisdom was something to behold . Harold was a torchbearer who was an inspiration to his peers and a great mentor and inspiration to so many musicians of all generations around the world for decades . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I feel fortunate to have known Harold and honoured to have shared the bandstand with him numerous times over the past five years . My deepest condolences to the Mabern family and Harold&amp;rsquo ; s extended musical family . Thank you Harold for the music and inspiration . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mabern played with MacDonald in Ottawa in the summer of 2015 . I was fortunate enough to interview him in advance of that gig , and below is the story that resulted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Harold Mabern was a late starter when it came to jazz piano , but he 's more than made up for it with his longevity . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Brooklyn-based pianist , 79 , didn&amp;rsquo ; t start playing his instrument until he was 15 , growing up in Memphis , Tennessee . But within a decade , the self-taught musician was accompanying the cream of the jazz crop in New York , right up to Miles Davis and Sonny Rollins , as well as singers Sarah Vaughan and Betty Carter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last decade , he&amp;rsquo ; s only grown busier , recording as a leader and sideman and touring , frequently with former students whom&amp;nbsp ; he taught at William Paterson University in New Jersey . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His appeal extends beyond the United States , such that Toronto saxophonist Kirk MacDonald enlisted Mabern to play on his most recent record , &lt;em&gt;Vista Obscura&lt;/em&gt; . This spring , that album won a Juno Award for best jazz album of the year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He&amp;rsquo ; s just such a great personality , &amp;rdquo ; MacDonald says of Mabern. &amp;ldquo ; His energy is infectious . It can be overwhelming &amp;hellip ; just his love for life and music . He&amp;rsquo ; s really one of the sweetest people you would ever meet . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacDonald and Mabern are touring&amp;nbsp ; this week and next , playing gigs from Sydney , N.S. , to Toronto . In Ottawa , the duo plays Salt Dining Lounge on Preston Street Saturday night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mabern , an effusive talker and storyteller who recently returned from a two-week tour in Japan , happily discussed his life as a jazzman ; an edited transcript of our&amp;nbsp ; conversation follows . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How did you get started playing piano ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m completely self-taught . No shame in my game . When I was 15 , I finally got a piano for about $50 , but I still really didn&amp;rsquo ; t know what I was doing . At the time , I think I knew about four songs . &amp;nbsp ; Unfortunately , I didn&amp;rsquo ; t have the luxury of playing Chopin Etudes. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Do you play classical music now ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oh no . Most jazz piano players play classical music but I don&amp;rsquo ; t fool with it . I do love classical music . Chopin is my favourite and Ravel and Debussy , and Gustav Mahler . But maybe one day I&amp;rsquo ; ll sit down and pick some notes out and see what I can do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How did you progress so quickly as a jazz pianist ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When I moved to Chicago , that&amp;rsquo ; s when I started putting time into it . In &amp;rsquo ; 54 , I moved to Chicago . &amp;nbsp ; Music was being played in Chicago 24/7. &amp;nbsp ; That&amp;rsquo ; s when I really learned how to play music . &amp;nbsp ; Music was on every corner . You had great pianists on every corner who could play . The camaraderie was very good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chicago , it&amp;rsquo ; s very diverse . You had to play all kinds of music . You had to play blues . You had to play for singers . I tell students the best way to be a great jazz pianist is to play for singers . That will teach you how to be a very good accompanist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Memphis , we had to play rhythm &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;rsquo ; blues . We didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to play rhythm &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;rsquo ; blues . We wanted to play bebop . And then as you look back , you say , &amp;ldquo ; Oh boy , what a pleasure , &amp;rdquo ; because as you know everybody can&amp;rsquo ; t play the blues . You can&amp;rsquo ; t teach that . It&amp;rsquo ; s something you can do or can&amp;rsquo ; t do and it has to do with the environment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Chicago , all the musicians could play anybody&amp;rsquo ; s music . How did that happen ? I don&amp;rsquo ; t know . We just listened . If Dizzy Gillespie came and Wynton Kelly , my hero , couldn&amp;rsquo ; t make it , I could go in and play the whole book without the music . If Bobby Timmons couldn&amp;rsquo ; t finish the gig with Art Blakey , I could play that music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We were playing and practising music 12 hours a day . I had a lot to do to get ready for&amp;nbsp ; New York . I&amp;rsquo ; m still getting ready for New York . You never stop learning . The day you stop being a student , you might as well pack it in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How did you start making a mark in New York ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; My first gig was with Harry &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; Edison. &amp;nbsp ; One night ( saxophonist ) Cannonball Adderley said , &amp;ldquo ; Hey , Big Hands , you want a gig ? &amp;rdquo ; He took me down to Birdland . Tuesday night , the place was packed . Must have been 15 piano players in Birdland that night . Cannonball introduced me to &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; says , &amp;ldquo ; You want to play ? &amp;rdquo ; I say &amp;ldquo ; yeah . &amp;rdquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m nervous . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I tell young students now , the old-timers assumed if you&amp;rsquo ; re a musician , you knew everything . They didn&amp;rsquo ; t ask you what you knew . So &amp;ldquo ; Sweets&amp;rdquo ; looked at me and he said , &amp;ldquo ; Habit . Eight-bar introduction . In A-flat. &amp;rdquo ; I thought , what&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; Habit ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The song was &lt;em&gt;You 're Getting To Be A Habit To Me&lt;/em&gt; . I fumbled the first chorus . The second chorus , I had it . He hired me on the spot . That was the first song I played in New York City . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;What was it like to play with Miles Davis in the early 1960s ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I can&amp;rsquo ; t believe that the six weeks I worked with Miles nobody had a tape recorder there to record ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a wonderful experience for me playing with Miles . He was very inspirational to me . He was saying ( imitates Davis&amp;rsquo ; raspy voice ) : &amp;ldquo ; You sure can swing . &amp;rdquo ; If Miles Davis was saying I can swing , that&amp;rsquo ; s all I needed to know . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;You 're coming to Canada right after a two-week tour of Japan . How was that ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We call New York the Big Apple , but as far as we&amp;rsquo ; re concerned , my friends and I , we say Tokyo , Japan , kicks New York&amp;rsquo ; s butt . First of all , there&amp;rsquo ; s 14 million people there . But the thing is , the people over there are so honest . Their mindset is they&amp;rsquo ; re so honourable , they take such great pride in their work . They treat you like a super-duper human being . I always tell people , in Japan they&amp;rsquo ; ve got money for two things : tall buildings and jazz . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; ve been over there 27 times . I love me some Tokyo , Japan , and when we come back to the United States , I get depressed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;How are you enjoying playing with Kirk MacDonald ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oh , love it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This date I did with him , that&amp;rsquo ; s probably the hardest record date that I&amp;rsquo ; ve ever done . He wrote some music , man . He&amp;rsquo ; s got one tune &amp;hellip ; I thought , &amp;ldquo ; If I can&amp;rsquo ; t hear this song , I can read it . &amp;rdquo ; I was up all night . It was only this one song , with a couple of bars I just couldn&amp;rsquo ; t get . Finally ( bassist ) Neil Swainson told me how to look at it and we were able to get two takes . I said , &amp;ldquo ; Sshh , don&amp;rsquo ; t nobody say a word because I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to have to do another . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; s a very challenging player . Excellent tenor player . It&amp;rsquo ; s definitely an enjoyable moment to play with him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;You 'll turn 80 next year . Do you expect you 'll slow down ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The only time I get tired is when I&amp;rsquo ; m not playing . I&amp;rsquo ; m blessed . I&amp;rsquo ; m booked up for the most part until April 2016 . As long as I feel I can work , let me work . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rest in peace , Harold Mabern. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;30305;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **36;30336;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //twitter.com/peterhum " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **30;30374;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;br&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **28;30406;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **30;30436;TOOLONG &; ... &lt;/p&gt; Peter HumFri , 20 Sep 2019 21:33:15 **45;30468;TOOLONG ... Plant plays CityFolk : ' I 'll probably retire when I run out of breath'http : **33;30515;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;30550;TOOLONG ... title= " Robert Plant . " alt= " Robert Plant . " data-portal-copyright= " mads perch " **28;30586;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1463799 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robert Plant &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; 9:15 p.m . Sunday , City Stage , CityFolk , Lansdowne Park &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tickets and info : &lt;a href= " http : //cityfolkfestival.com " &gt;cityfol ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;When it comes to working into one 's sunset years , Robert Plant relates more to Bob Dylan than Elton John . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Although the 71-year-old rock legend expressed the utmost respect for his fellow Brit , John , who 's 72 and circumnavigating the globe on an epic farewell tour , the former Led Zeppelin frontman said he 's nowhere near ready to follow his footsteps . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " Elton is looking for more time to spend with his family , and that 's not a bad idea , " Plant said in an interview . " But my family always says , ' Keep going , Dad . ' &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " Elton has his own ideas and everybody does , but I have n't actually split the atom . I have n't reached a point where I 've got nothing left , and I feel pretty good about what I do . I love to tour and I love to sing and twist the songs around . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;It 's a mindset that puts him more in tune with 78-year-old Dylan , who once gave Plant the advice to keep going as long as possible . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " I respect and admire him , not only for waking me up as a kid , a teenager , but also for the fact that he takes such joy in re-entering his own adventures from a different angle . He works more than I do , I think , " he said . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Plant also recalled a conversation with Dylan when they were both playing in the same region of Spain . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " ' Did you know there are 46 bullfighting rings in Spain that you can play ? ' " Dylan asked him . " ' You got ta keep going . ' &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Play , and let the music take you to fine people and introduce you to the underbelly of all these areas ' cultures . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " I 'll probably retire when I run out of breath . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;That does n't seem to be happening any time soon . Plant 's most recent album , 2017 's Carry Fire , recorded with his Sensational Space Shifters band , finds him at a creative peak . His voice is as strong and supple as ever , and the music brims with vitality , meandering purposefully between psychedelia , folk and blues . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;To get to that point , he feels it 's important to leave space for spontaneity in the studio . He does n't want to have things too mapped out . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " It 's pretty nebulous and non-definable , clutching stuff and bringing it out of the air , and laying it down in some form or another with instruments , " says the musical mastermind . " It takes a myriad of different forms to get to the point in the song where you think you 've actually got it . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " There 's an air of adventure , and I think with contemporary recording techniques , you can create impressions as a song develops its stature and its shape takes place . You can hang all sorts of different ideas on it as it grows and blossoms . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;His creative process often involves showing up to the session early . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " I go in fresh every morning . Sometimes I gave myself an hour or two before the guys came in to work on various tracks , and just moved things around , switched stuff about . Really , it 's almost like a watercolour painting . You may actually add another colour before the colour is dry for a different effect . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Plant has always been an artist who prefers to talk about his newest material instead of revisiting the past . But he was encouraged by his label to explore his five-decade output in a new podcast that debuted this year . Each episode of Digging Deep with Robert Plant finds the singer-songwriter talking to a journalist about a specific song . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " I always deal in the current , present tense , and I 'm only ever really getting excited about what I 'm actually doing right now so I never thought about the idea of visiting the stuff again , " he says . " But every song has got a tale , and when I think about all the previous albums , all of them had some kind of attitude , whether it was trying to grasp the 80s or courting technology or not meeting with the approval of the Led Zeppelin fans at large . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " So I listen to the songs and think , ' Wow , those guys were great players . ' It 's nice to look at that stuff . I like the idea of visiting tracks with an interest in them . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;As for the current tour , Plant is looking forward to bringing the Sensational Space Shifters to Canada , though there are just two dates this time , Ottawa 's CityFolk and Fredericton 's Harvest Jazz and Blues Festival , before he continues into the U.S. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " I only go where I want to go , " says the Rock ' n ' Roll Hall of Famer . " I remember playing Winnipeg and , I think , Calgary in Zeppelin . Towns carry the personality of the weather , the climate and the culture swings with the seasons . Some regions are tough to live in and they have a different kind of crowd . The audience has a different kind of tenacity about them . Canada is riveting really because it 's quite dynamic along that long , long journey from east to west . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; lsaxberg@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN ARTS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;30616;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " David Kawai " **28;30839;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1259892 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Rock legend Robert Plant , feel-good hitmakers The Strumbellas , Smooth Sailin ' soulman Leon Bridges and a triple header of 90s-rock outfits are headed for the main stage at this weekend 's edition of Ottawa 's CityFolk festival . The fun starts Thursday , Sept. 12 and runs to Sunday , Sept. 15 at Lansdowne Park . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The festival features performers on two stages : the main , outdoor City Stage on Lansdowne 's Great Lawn , and the RavenLaw Stage , located inside Aberdeen Pavilion . There 's also an on-site food court , merch table , craft beverages by Mill Street Brewery and a kids ' zone that will be in operation Saturday and Sunday . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Of course , the big news is Plant 's return to Ottawa for the first time since 2011 , when the Led Zep frontman appeared at the Ottawa Jazz Festival . This time , he brings his Sensational Space Shifters band and the psychedelic folky-blues of his 2017 Carry Fire album . They close the festival on Sept. 15. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30869;TOOLONG ... title= " People watch from the vantage point of a grass-covered slope as Whitehorse performs on the last evening of CityFolk at Lansdowne Park on Sunday , Sept. 16 , 2018. " alt= " People watch from the vantage point of a grass-covered slope as Whitehorse performs on the last evening of CityFolk at Lansdowne Park on Sunday , Sept. 16 , 2018. " data-portal-copyright= " David Kawai " **28;30905;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1259892 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;But first , leading up to that auspicious finale is an eclectic mix of musical entertainment , starting with the eagerly anticipated return of one of Canada 's most successful exports , The Strumbellas . The Ontario band made an all-too- brief appearance on Parliament Hill as part of this summer 's Canada Day lineup , whetting appetites for their full set at CityFolk on Thursday . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Opening night also includes main-stage performances by Americana queen Lucinda Williams and masked troubadour Orville Peck , along with RavenLaw stage sets by Newfoundland folk trio The Once , roots-rock sisters Larkin Poe and Toronto party band Dwayne Gretzky. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Dig out your plaid shirt and combat boots to relive the grunge era on Friday , with a live soundtrack supplied by Canadian rockers Our Lady Peace and their American counterparts in Live and Bush . Dear Rouge is also on the main stage , while the secondary stage on Friday features singer-songwriter Donovan Woods and the cinematic spectacle of electropop fave Rich Aucoin. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The 30-year-old American soul man , Leon Bridges , headlines Saturday 's main stage , with support by Hey Rosetta ! frontman Tim Baker , soft-pop duo Rhye and songwriter-poet Saul Williams . Highlights of the RavenLaw schedule that day include California 's Eleni Mandell , Ontario rocker Lee Harvey Osmond and a special tribute to Ottawa 's songwriting legend , David Wiffen , featuring a who's-who of the local scene . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The festival wraps up on Sunday with a diverse bill , topped by Plant , but also including main-stage shows by folk-rockers The East Pointers , heart-tugging American singer-songwriter Lucy Dacus and California roots rockers Dawes . Headed to the RavenLaw stage are L.A. road warrior The White Buffalo and Canuck folk duo Harrow Fair , as well as afternoon showcases by the Bluesfest music school 's ukulele camp and rock university . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;30935;TOOLONG ... title= " FILE : Fans at the CityFolk Festival . " alt= " FILE : Fans at the CityFolk Festival . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;30971;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1462614 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;General-admission passes for the entire weekend are $160 ( taxes included ) . Day passes , pick-two passes and VIP day passes are also available . VIP weekend passes are sold out . Go to cityfolkfestival.com for tickets and schedule information . Children nine and under are admitted free with a ticket-holding adult . The festival runs rain or shine . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;In addition to the musical riches presented on the main site , CityFolk 's offshoot festival , Marvest , once again takes over the shops , bars and restaurants of Bank Street . Dozens of acts from Ottawa and beyond will play free shows in clubs such as Irene 's Pub , but also in storefronts that do n't usually feature live music , on Sept. 13 and 14 . Sunday , Canadian Tire Centre &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tickets : Start at $77.40 , including taxes and fees , available at&amp;nbsp ; &lt;a href= " https : //slack-redir.net/link ? url=http%3 ... target= " blank " class= " c-link " rel= " noopener " **29;31288;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Pickering , Ont. pop singer-songwriter Shawn Mendes arrives at the &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;Canadian Tire Centre for a show on Aug. 18 as part of his new self-titled tour . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His tour was announced so long ago &amp;mdash ; way back in May 2018 &amp;mdash ; the now 21-year-old Mendes was still a literal teen idol at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 2018 was a big month for Mendes , as it also marked the release of his eponymous third album . Featuring hits In My Blood and Se&amp;ntilde ; orita , Shawn Mendes : The Album stretched his musical muscles beyond his humble acoustic guitar beginnings without threatening his wholesome image , with forays into glossier R&amp;amp ; B and adult pop ( along the lines of renowned style dabbler John Mayer ) . The slick release topped the Billboard album charts both at home and in the United States , where it also nabbed a Best Pop Vocal Album Grammy nomination . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h2 class= " cat-head short " **40;31319;TOOLONG ... &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;31361;TOOLONG ... is Justin Stirling ? Meet the newest member of the Shawn Mendes management **25;31396;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here are five things to know about Shawn Mendes in advance of his CTC show on Sunday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;31423;TOOLONG ... title= " Canadian singer-songwriter Shawn Mendes performed Saturday July 7 , 2018 , at RBC Bluesfest on the City Stage . " alt= " Canadian singer-songwriter Shawn Mendes performed Saturday July 7 , 2018 , at RBC Bluesfest on the City Stage . " data-portal-copyright= " Ashley Fraser " **28;31459;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1227917 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;1 . A social media success story&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The old story about unknown bands getting discovered by jaded A&amp;amp ; Rs in dimly lit venues is mostly a thing of the past . From his sleepy Toronto suburb , Mendes developed a following the modern way : posting acoustic guitar covers on defunct social media app Vine , famous for its hard six-second time limit . His elevator pitch worked , as he amassed millions of followers , a manager and label deal with Island Records thanks to his online exploits . That was in 2014 &amp;ndash ; today , Mendes is approaching a whopping 50 million followers on Instagram , putting him alongside compatriots Justin Bieber and Drake among the most-followed Instagram accounts on Earth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;2 . A star-laden affair&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The latest album from Mendes offers a who&amp;rsquo ; s who of young pop stars and behind-the-scenes hitmakers . Se&amp;ntilde ; orita features American singer Camila Cabello , and the rumoured couple is set to perform the song together at the MTV VMAs on Aug 26 . El Paso singer Khalid guests on Youth . Fallin&amp;rsquo ; All in You features a co-write from none other than Ed Sheeran , while Like to Be You was produced by John Mayer . Ryan Tedder of OneRepublic , Julia Michaels and Teddy Geiger &amp;ndash ; all mainstays on the singles charts as songwriters &amp;ndash ; also have their fingerprints on this release . Mendes giveth as well , guesting on Chance the Rapper&amp;rsquo ; s new album The Big Day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;31489;TOOLONG ... title= " Camila Cabello. " alt= " Camila Cabello. " data-portal-copyright= " Rodin Eckenroth " **28;31525;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1447787 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;3 . A little plain and that 's OK&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pop stardom demands burning the candle at both ends , but the clean cut Mendes comes across as an early to bed type . His plainness in a world of provocateurs has not gone unnoticed : The Guardian called him &amp;ldquo ; perfect boyfriend material in pop&amp;rsquo ; s all important fantasy world , &amp;rdquo ; comparing him favourably to &amp;ldquo ; unknowable loose cannon&amp;rdquo ; Bieber . And in Mendes&amp;rsquo ; Rolling Stone cover story , which presented him as a &amp;ldquo ; neurotic pop idol , &amp;rdquo ; his father Manny and manager discussed his apparent lack of game with the ladies . &amp;ldquo ; One day , brother . Maybe now that I&amp;rsquo ; m 20 , &amp;rdquo ; Mendes responded in the article , then adding : &amp;ldquo ; I said &amp;lsquo ; f&amp;ndash ; k&amp;rsquo ; onstage the other day . That was a big day . I immediately felt bad about it . &amp;rdquo ; In the same interview he professed his love of weed , but even that&amp;rsquo ; s pretty tame as far as pop star antics go . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;4 . Beloved back home&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mendes might be more famous for being a spokesperson and model for Calvin Klein underwear , but nothing drives home your Canadian-ness quite like having your own limited edition line of Roots clothing , which he did . And while he may have received two Grammy nominations and performed on music&amp;rsquo ; s biggest night alongside Miley Cyrus , Mendes cleaned up at this year&amp;rsquo ; s Junos in his native land , winning album of the year , single of the year , artist of the year , pop album of the year and songwriter of the year . In addition to Ottawa , his current world tour includes stops in Vancouver , Edmonton , Saskatoon , Winnipeg , Montreal and Toronto , where he&amp;rsquo ; ll end the tour&amp;rsquo ; s North American leg at Rogers Centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;5 . A Montreal remix&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Album single Where Were You in the Morning ? received a smooth remix treatment by Montreal producer Kaytranada last December . The new version , available on a three-song EP that also includes Leon Bridges and Canadian Jessie Reyez adding their touches to Mendes tracks , neatly combines Kaytranada&amp;rsquo ; s signature floating synths and percussion with Mendes&amp;rsquo ; heartthrob falsetto . The pair fit well together , giving one hope that there might be a little quid pro quo when Kaytranada&amp;rsquo ; s next album sees the light of day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;31555;TOOLONG ... " **28;31917;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1445788 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;43-year-old musician , who 's in Ottawa this week for the first time to play a gig at the Rainbow Bistro , started her music education as a conservatory-trained violinist destined for orchestra life . She grew up in Kuopio , a small town in eastern Finland , a country with a surprisingly vibrant blues scene . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Classical &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " style= " font-family : -apple-system , BlinkMacSystemFont , ' Segoe UI ' , Roboto , Oxygen-Sans , Ubuntu , Cantarell , ' Helvetica Neue ' , sans-serif ; " &gt;music did n't interest me as a teenager , " Lyytinen said .
