King cancels Bluesfest date due to medical emergency&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Lynn Saxberg , Aedan HelmerThu , 11 Jul 2019 11:40:15 **45;36306;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicBluesfest preview : Kane Brown on finding success on the charts and at homehttp : **36;36353;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;36391;TOOLONG ... title= " Kane Brown . " alt= " Kane Brown . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;36427;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425793 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;36457;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kane Brown&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thursday , 930-10:45 p.m. , City Stage &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; RBC Bluesfest , LeBreton Flats Park &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kane Brown is one of the most anticipated headliners at RBC Bluesfest and an explosive success in country music &amp;mdash ; but the 25-year-old is still getting used to it all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; So much has happened . I still feel like I&amp;rsquo ; m new . And when I tell people I still feel new , they&amp;rsquo ; re like , &amp;lsquo ; What do you mean you feel new ? &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; the American Music Award-winning artist said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For someone who considers himself a fresh face , Brown has already seen considerable success . Five years ago , he was a kid from Chattanooga , Tennessee posting country covers to Facebook and picking up followers in droves . Now , in his four years with the recording industry , Brown has two EPs , two albums and a slew of singles under his belt . His latest album , Experiment , debuted at No. 1 on the Billboard charts last fall . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And it&amp;rsquo ; s clear he still has lots of room to make bigger waves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The recent changes in Brown&amp;rsquo ; s life have been many and frequent &amp;mdash ; both on a personal and a career level . Last month , he featured on a track with the hit-making DJ and producer Marshmello in the song One Thing Right . And last fall , just before Experiment&amp;rsquo ; s release , he married fellow musician Katelyn Jae . The couple announced Jae was pregnant last spring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Critics have praised Experiment for its robust lineup of strong material , and for blending its country sound with rock , pop and even RnB . Brown spent much of his youth listening to RnB , and only made his commitment to country music later in high school . But for all of his diverse influences , his sound and themes are still decidedly country , which distinguishes him in a genre where new artists are increasingly latching onto other styles . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; For me , I want to try and get back to the traditionalist country fans . I get it , my music&amp;rsquo ; s not traditional country &amp;mdash ; whose is , right now ? For me it was like , that was kind of my way , that if they wanted to listen to my music , they get to hear some of the old sounds that they don&amp;rsquo ; t hear much anymore . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brown also keeps tabs on the business side of his music . &amp;nbsp ; He likes to keep track of the stats for individual songs on streaming platforms , and said data can influence how he prepares a concert . &amp;ldquo ; I look at all my numbers , and if it&amp;rsquo ; s not streaming well , I take it out of my set , &amp;rdquo ; he said , naming American Bad Dream , an album track about gun violence in schools ( and the closest he&amp;rsquo ; s gotten to political ) , as one example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of One Thing Right , he said that &amp;ldquo ; streaming-wise , I think it&amp;rsquo ; s my fastest-streaming song so far . &amp;rdquo ; The collab with Marshmello came together when the DJ reached out to Brown after the AMAs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I already had this song from my album that didn&amp;rsquo ; t make the cut , but it was one of my favourite songs that I&amp;rsquo ; d written , &amp;rdquo ; he said of One Thing Right . &amp;ldquo ; Just wasn&amp;rsquo ; t right for the album . It was a hard choice , but we had to just leave it off . Honestly , I&amp;rsquo ; m kind of glad I did , I&amp;rsquo ; m glad Marshmello picked it up . I feel like it&amp;rsquo ; s a song a lot of people in the world can relate to , and he&amp;rsquo ; s got a way bigger fanbase than what I&amp;rsquo ; ve got . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brown built his own fanbase through social media , posting simple cellphone videos of himself singing country covers with his friends . But he said his relationship with social media has changed alongside his career . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m bigger than I&amp;rsquo ; ve ever been , which is how career works , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; When it comes to social media , man , you just start seeing a lot more hate . I had to kind of get away from it for a while . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was so open when I got out there , I feel like that&amp;rsquo ; s what connected me with my fans . But now it&amp;rsquo ; s just , the more open I am with people , the more I feel like I&amp;rsquo ; m judged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I care less now &amp;mdash ; people can say something about me and I just brush it off my shoulder . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brown said his journey to stardom was not easy , but that he&amp;rsquo ; s now happy where he&amp;rsquo ; s at in life . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I got to live the hard life , not really having anything , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I always got picked on growing up , and I got to live through all the hard stuff , so now it&amp;rsquo ; s like I&amp;rsquo ; m living my life &amp;mdash ; a great life &amp;mdash ; got a beautiful wife , a baby on the way ; everything&amp;rsquo ; s going good for me , so why should I have to worry about anything ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brown plays RBC Bluesfest on Thursday , the day before premiering his new single , Like a Rodeo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;36493;TOOLONG ... preview : Alexisonfire back with first new music in a decade&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;36528;TOOLONG ... Bluesfest daily picks : Logic and Elle King&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " http : **34;36564;TOOLONG ... Review : Buddy Guy , Sue Foley put the ' blues ' in Bluesfest&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Jacob HoytemaWed , 10 Jul 2019 16:54:34 **45;36600;TOOLONG ... Review : Buddy Guy , Sue Foley put the ' blues ' in Bluesfesthttp : **31;36647;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;36680;TOOLONG ... title= " Sue Foley on the Videotron Stage as day 5 of the RBC Bluesfest takes place on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum . " alt= " Sue Foley on the Videotron Stage as day 5 of the RBC Bluesfest takes place on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;36716;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1426120 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;36746;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Buddy Guy / Sue **30;36782;TOOLONG &lt;strong&gt;RBC Ottawa **34;36814;TOOLONG **36;36850;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;Bluesfest gave its long-serving faithful what they 've been asking for all these years with a double-shot of true blues Tuesday as hometown hero Sue Foley heated up the stage for the long-awaited return of the legend himself , Buddy Guy . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The 82-year-old ( soon to be 83 ) Louisiana-born bluesman wore a wide smile as he strutted out on the Vid ? otron Stage on the festival 's quieter north side -- with an older and much mellower crowd than the one that turned up for KYGO on the main stage -- wearing his trademark black-and-white polka-dot shirt and his well-worn Stratocaster slung over his shoulder . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;He launched straight into Damn Right , I 've Got the Blues , from the 1991 album that cemented Guy 's stature among the best of the legendary electric Chicago bluesmen. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " Did I play that too loud ? " he half-apologized as his solo screamed to a crescendo. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " No ! " the crowd shot back as Guy 's smile grew wider . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " That 's good , because I 'm gon na get loud , " he pledged . " I can also play something so funky you can smell it ! " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;36888;TOOLONG ... title= " Buddy Guy performs at RBC Bluesfest on Tuesday , July 9 , 2019 " alt= " Buddy Guy performs at RBC Bluesfest on Tuesday , July 9 , 2019 " data-portal-copyright= " Photo by Sean Sisk " **28;36924;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1426188 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;He was damn right about that , too , kicking off the slinky bass riff of Willie Dixon 's I 'm Your Hoochie Coochie Man . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The only problem was it might not have been quite loud enough , as that song 's slow-building and deliberate intro was nearly drowned out by the throbbing electro bass beats of KYGO wafting over the hillside from the main stage on the opposite end of LeBreton Flats . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt; " I taught myself to play guitar , and I listened to a lot of the great bluesmen who are no longer with us , " he said during a mid-set break . " I got to make a lot of records with those guys , and we all promised each other that whoever is left would keep the blues alive . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;36954;TOOLONG ... title= " Buddy Guy performs at RBC Bluesfest on Tuesday , July 9 , 2019 " alt= " Buddy Guy performs at RBC Bluesfest on Tuesday , July 9 , 2019 " data-portal-copyright= " Sean Sisk " **28;36990;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1426187 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;He did just that , reeling off a medley of his favourites , from the classic Boom Boom by John Lee Hooker -- " As far as I 'm concerned the greatest guitar player I ever heard , " Guy said -- to classic cuts from B.B King and Albert King . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;And Guy did n't stop there . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;One of the first bluesmen to truly embrace the British wave of longhaired Sixties rock n ' rollers -- who largely built their early careers by ripping riffs straight out of that same classic blues catalogue -- Guy demonstrated exactly how those sounds were born when he teased out Cream 's Sunshine of Your Love ( using a drum stick to smash out the classic rock riff ) , and sprinkling in bits of The Rolling Stones ' Miss You for good measure before landing on an inspired sing-along run through Take Me to the River . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;The music must keep him young , as Guy was looking spry as he surveyed the crowd -- many of them likely around the Bluesfest scene when he first played the festival back in 1995 , an edition where Guy also happened to share the stage with Sue Foley . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;37020;TOOLONG ... title= " Sue Foley on the Videotron Stage as day 5 of the RBC Bluesfest takes place on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum . " alt= " Sue Foley on the Videotron Stage as day 5 of the RBC Bluesfest takes place on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;37056;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1426136 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Taking the Vid&amp;eacute ; otron Stage for the evening set , Foley was radiant in a studded white blazer and dark shades , with her trademark pink paisley Telecaster and her orange hair flashing in the burning sunset off the Ottawa River . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Opening with the straight-up Texas boogie of Run , a tune flush with the unmistakable sound of her guitar hero Albert Collins , Foley kept it pure and simple when it came to her solo , favouring that &amp;ldquo ; power of one note&amp;rdquo ; that Collins famously employed over the flashier soloists who trip up and down the fretboard. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With rocksteady Toronto-based band mates Tom Bona on drums and Leo Valvasori on bass , Foley alternated between chugging out chunky power chords and ripping out crystal-clear electric guitar lines . Never content to simply just land on a note , Foley instead digs in , stretches out and milks that note for all it&amp;rsquo ; s worth before moving on to the next flurry . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She promised to play plenty from The Ice Queen , her latest album on the famed Stony Plain label , and a warning for the crowd as she introduced Fool&amp;rsquo ; s Gold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Don&amp;rsquo ; t believe everything you see or everything you hold &amp;hellip ; It might just be fool&amp;rsquo ; s gold . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;37086;TOOLONG ... title= " Sue Foley on the Videotron Stage as day 5 of the RBC Bluesfest takes place on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum . " alt= " Sue Foley on the Videotron Stage as day 5 of the RBC Bluesfest takes place on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum . " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;37122;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1426135 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was pure gold as the slow boogie unfolded with Foley grinding out some stinging lines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The album&amp;rsquo ; s title track was a highlight for the locals &amp;mdash ; Foley mentioned she and her band mates had plenty of friends and family in the crowd &amp;mdash ; though it would have been easy for anyone there to recognize the setting as she sang about hailing from a place &amp;ldquo ; where the ground stays frozen for more than half the year . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But if there were any ice floes still straggling on the Ottawa River , they would have instantly melted from the heat emanating off the evening stage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ice Queen featured Foley&amp;rsquo ; s first real extended solo , and she made the absolute most of it with her hybrid of thumb-and-fingerpicking &amp;mdash ; instead of using a guitar pick &amp;mdash ; that gives each note an alternating soft , brushing sound or a driving attack . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The odd styling made perfect sense when Foley explained she used to take flamenco guitar lessons in Ottawa , and showed off a bit of what she incorporated into her own blues style on The Dance , followed by the traditional Cannonball Blues , a tribute to Mother Maybelle Carter and all the great blueswomen who continue to inspire her . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She&amp;rsquo ; s the one inspiring others now , though , and she showed why , inviting her pals in the Texas Horns to close things out with a furious double-shot of Texas blues on The Lucky Ones and Gaslight. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span class= " s1 " &gt;&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37152;TOOLONG ... Bluesfest daily picks : Lennon Stella and Sue Foley&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37187;TOOLONG ... the headliners : Who to watch for the second week of Bluesfest&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37222;TOOLONG ... preview : Elle King on making music during the toughest time of her life&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; Aedan HelmerWed , 10 Jul 2019 16:30:55 **45;37257;TOOLONG ... ReviewsMusicBluesfest preview : Alexisonfire back with first new music in a decadehttp : **34;37304;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;37340;TOOLONG ... title= " Alexisonfire. " alt= " Alexisonfire. " data-portal-copyright= " HANDOUT " **28;37376;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425728 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;37406;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; **41;37442;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;9:30-11 p.m . Friday , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;City Stage&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; RBC Ottawa Bluesfest , LeBreton Flats Park &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alexisonfire is back , and not just as a festival headliner but also as a creative entity . The post-hardcore band from St. Catharines , which broke up in 2011 when guitarist Dallas Green made the decision to focus on his City and Colour side project , has been testing the waters of the music industry again , and finding them rather stimulating , according to lead singer George Pettit. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New material is bubbling up and two searing new songs , Complicit and Familiar Drugs , have been released so far this year . Although there&amp;rsquo ; s no firm plan for a full album &amp;mdash ; yet &amp;mdash ; Pettit doesn&amp;rsquo ; t rule it out . In this interview ( edited for length and clarity ) , the 36-year-old also talks about his full-time gig as an Oshawa firefighter , how he keeps his pulverizing voice in shape and the curse of pre-show anxiety . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **34;37485;TOOLONG ... title= " Alexisonfire. " alt= " Alexisonfire. " data-portal-copyright= " HANDOUT " **28;37521;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1425728 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What prompted you guys to get back together and write some new songs ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Well , we 've been playing shows on and off for the last few years , accepting offers to play festivals , and spirits were high in the camp . We are all still pretty close , and so I remember sending out an email , ( saying ) , ' We 've been doing this for a little while , playing shows , and I 'd like to continue , and in order to do that , we got ta probably write some music . ' &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;It 's a tough thing for us to do because aligning all the schedules is very difficult . We all have our own lives outside of the band . But it got easier when Dallas moved back to Toronto from Nashville , and we started going to his place for regular practices and we started writing material . So then we did a quick little session in southern Ontario , just recorded as much as we could in that time . And we 've been trickling it out as the months go on this year . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : So your strategy now is to release them one at a time . Will there be an album ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : I do n't think anybody in this band is that strategic but loosely , I 'd say that is what 's going on . The future is still very much unwritten for Alexis . I would assume at some point there will be more music , but we have n't had that big conversation yet about what it 's going to be or if we 're all going to upset anything in our lives in order to write an album or do anything like that . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;We 're just kind of enjoying the moment and enjoying playing together . And releasing songs : that 's how people consume music now . I 'm a bit of a romantic in that I do like an album , but a lot of people are listening to playlists more than they are to albums. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How does it feel to be back on stage ? Do fans remember Alexisonfire ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , there 's more fans now than there were back in the heyday of Alexis . We 're playing some of the biggest shows we 've ever played and it 's good . I do n't play enough now where it 's an old-hat type of thing , which is nice . I get nervous , and I get anxiety before the show . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;I 'm a mess ahead of the show because we do n't play all the time anymore . Towards the end of Alexis , I 'd go on stage like I was putting my pants on . No big deal . I get nervous and antsy , but the fear kinda propels you to play better . I find it easier to get to a place of performance when you got a little bit of nerves in your stomach . It 's been really fun but not without a bit of anxiety and a bit of a worry . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : I understand you became a firefighter during the Alexisonfire downtime . Why did you decide to pursue that occupation ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Well , my band broke up and I had to do something . I think I wanted something that was going to get me off the road . The obvious thing to do would have been to get back into the music industry and do something like be a tour manager , but I just thought that fire-fighting would give me some sort of job stability . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;I looked into it and started talking to people I know who had friends who were firefighters . I made myself a fairly lengthy checklist of all the things I needed to do to become a firefighter , and spent three-and-a-half years doing all that stuff , then started applying to departments . It was a long journey but then I got hired in Oshawa and I 've been there ever since . It 's a very rewarding career , and it 's a fairly flexible schedule . It 's nice that I can do both and it 's not an issue at all . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Very cool . Have you been recognized when you 're out on a call ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Maybe twice . I 'm not that recognizable , to tell you the truth , and when I 'm in uniform , people are n't expecting to see me . I 'm not that famous really . I do n't think Alexisonfire is the type of band you look at pictures of very often , and now that MuchMusic is pretty much dead , we do n't really get a lot of screen time . I do appreciate a certain degree of anonymity . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Was it a challenge to get your voice back in shape for performing ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : No , not really . I have n't had an issue with my voice since probably 2003 . I lost my voice once when we were on tour , and I came back , healed up and then we went back on the road , and I never lost it again . I do almost no vocal warmups . I do things I probably should n't do for my voice all the time . And it just keeps doing what it does . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Having said that , I do n't necessarily have to hit fine notes . I 'm just belting it out . My voice is more like a jackhammer than a scalpel . I just never had an issue . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Touring can be a challenge for a band . How is it now for you ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : It 's nice now that my little guy is getting older and he flies really well . And my wife enjoys it . I brought my family to Australia with Alexisonfire , and New York and L.A. so I get to kinda experience touring again through the eyes of a child . When you have traveled as much as Alexisonfire , you can get jaded about the idea of travel so it 's nice having people around who are still bewildered by new places , and excited about them . I really appreciate bringing them on the road with me. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : It must feel like a new chapter for the band . &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;A : Yeah , it 's like the band is starting over , honestly . It 's a whole new experience , which is really cool . We found a way to do it , and try to do it as stress-free as possible so we 're not banging our heads against the pavement anymore . We 're not touring for 10 months out of the year . We 're playing in a band with self-care in mind , and we still very much like to keep grounded in a world outside the music industry as well . I think we 're in a good place . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; lsaxberg@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37551;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicTravel woes force cancellation of Colton Wall Bluesfest showhttp : **36;37703;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;37741;TOOLONG ... title= " The crowds roll in as the 25th anniversary edition of RBC Bluesfest gets underway on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum in Lebreton Flats . Wayne Cuddington/ Postmedia " alt= " The crowds roll in as the 25th anniversary edition of RBC Bluesfest gets underway on the grounds of the Canadian War Museum in Lebreton Flats . Wayne Cuddington/ Postmedia " data-portal-copyright= " Wayne Cuddington " **28;37777;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1423732 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Unforeseen travel complications have forced country artist Colton Wall to cancel his performance at Friday night&amp;rsquo ; s edition of RBC Bluesfest. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wall had been due to perform on the City Stage at 6 p.m. , followed by Jason Isbell and the 400 Unit at 7:30 p.m. and headliner Eric Church at 9:30 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was no immediate word on a replacement or whether Wall will perform on another day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Due to unforeseen travel complications , Colter Wall will not be able to perform at RBC Bluesfest this evening . &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;For the most up-to-date schedule , visit &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/cUWwuQABcE " &gt;https : //t.c ... or download our mobile app ! &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/wPOC2ZtmZq " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- RBC Bluesfest Ottawa ( @ottawabluesfest ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;37807;TOOLONG ... 5 , **33;37842;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;37877;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO IN THE NEWS : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37912;TOOLONG ... review : Chvrches , alt-J add heat to steamy opening night&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37947;TOOLONG ... day at Bluesfest : 10 days of beverages , sunscreen and live music&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;37982;TOOLONG ... your minister and you 're a f -- ing loser ' : Eugene Melnyk recounts Lisa MacLeod 's alleged public tirade&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Staff ReporterSat , 06 Jul 2019 00:15:34 **45;38017;TOOLONG ... NewsMusicBluesfest preview Q&amp;amp ; A : Elle King on making music during the toughest time of her lifehttp : **36;38064;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;38102;TOOLONG ... title= " Elle King . " alt= " Elle King . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;38138;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1423460 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;38168;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elle King has covered a wide musical landscape , and a lot of emotional ground along her rapid rise to fame after breaking out with her 2014 smash hit Ex&amp;rsquo ; s and Oh&amp;rsquo ; s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With a guitar-slinging , banjo-toting style that crosses over from rock to country to pop &amp;mdash ; influenced equally by Earl Scruggs and AC/DC &amp;mdash ; King and her band The Brethren are riding high again with 2018&amp;rsquo ; s Shake the Spirit , recorded through a turbulent time following a whirlwind romance , marriage and divorce . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve never set out to make one cohesive record , I&amp;rsquo ; m not that kind of artist , &amp;rdquo ; King said in an interview as her tour rolls into Bluesfest Wednesday . &amp;ldquo ; I sing , like five different genres in 20 minutes . I just have so many different influences and I wake up in a different musical mood every day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not that I can&amp;rsquo ; t put myself in a box , it&amp;rsquo ; s that nobody&amp;rsquo ; s ever tried . Honestly I&amp;rsquo ; d like to see somebody try . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How did the ups and downs of the last couple of years inform the music , lyrics and emotions of Shake the Spirit ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : I was going through a lot in my personal life , and I didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to make a record . They kinda said , &amp;lsquo ; You have to make a record . &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And so I just said , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m gon na do it my way , &amp;rsquo ; because I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t physically or emotionally do it any other way . I tried working with ( other methods ) &amp;mdash ; I went to one songwriting session and the person made me cry because they wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t let me express myself , so I was like , &amp;lsquo ; Alright , f&amp;ndash ; k this . &amp;rsquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I couldn&amp;rsquo ; t really leave my house so my bandmates would just take turns flying out to California , and all by chance we found a studio in Texas , and it was one of those totally meant-to-be situations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So we made a record through the toughest time in my life . And I really do feel that because I had this creative outlet during this time , I left all that behind me and I really came out of it a different person . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : There 's a real sense of catharsis , of shedding all this baggage , that goes back to your first hit Ex 's and Oh 's . Is that a challenge to get up in front of strangers and pour your soul out , or is that part of the catharsis ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : Every show is different , some shows I feel more comfortable and more safe than others . Sometimes I&amp;rsquo ; ll turn around and I will cut a song like Man&amp;rsquo ; s Man because that one is just a lot for me to sing . I put them on the record and they tell a story , but I&amp;rsquo ; m a human being and I can get inside my own head . So sometimes I won&amp;rsquo ; t play a certain song , but that&amp;rsquo ; s my right , I think . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But I can distance myself from those songs because when I wrote them and when we recorded them , I really left a lot of emotions there . Most songs I can just enjoy them for what they are , and feel proud that I made something through a lot of pain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : You 've been very honest with fans through social media , but is that connection double-edged in that fans will expect you to live up to a certain image ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : No , I think expectations actually went down &amp;mdash ; I really had a hard time living up to the Elle that my fans thought was the big partier . That was so much more pressure . And people thought maybe I was a bit more fragile ( during my divorce ) , so a lot of my fans came to support . So I never felt that pressure . I felt a lot of pressure a couple years ago to get f&amp;mdash ; -d up , but I&amp;rsquo ; ve changed a lot and that&amp;rsquo ; s not my No. 1 priority in life . I feel like people respect that . I&amp;rsquo ; ve been very open and honest about where I&amp;rsquo ; m at in my life and my fans appreciate that . Mainly they just want to know that I&amp;rsquo ; m OK . I have very loving fans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : The rock n ' roll lifestyle can take a toll . Is there anything you would go back and tell the Elle of three years ago ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A : The rock n&amp;rsquo ; roll music biz , and doing drugs and partying might be the some of the most fun you could ever f&amp;mdash ; ing have . But it&amp;rsquo ; ll either kill you , or it&amp;rsquo ; ll kill your career real fast . So it all depends on what you want out of your life . I feel very thankful , I wake up in the morning one day and say , &amp;lsquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not gon na do that any more , &amp;rsquo ; and I don&amp;rsquo ; t do it any more . Other people struggle . It&amp;rsquo ; s a really dangerous game . Having fun and partying , it&amp;rsquo ; s literally Russian Roulette . So it&amp;rsquo ; s hard for me to give advice , because I never took it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elle King &lt;br&gt; RBC Ottawa Bluesfest &lt;br&gt; Wed . July 10 &lt;br&gt; Videotron Stage , 9:30 p.m. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; ahelmer@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Twitter.com/helmera &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;ALSO : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;38204;TOOLONG ... review : Chvrches , alt-J add heat to steamy opening night&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;38239;TOOLONG ... day at Bluesfest : 10 days of beverages , sunscreen and live music&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;38274;TOOLONG ... Bluesfest picks for the first weekend of the festival&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Aedan HelmerFri , 05 Jul 2019 12:26:50 **45;38309;TOOLONG ... guide to Canada Day music on the Hill , including The Strumbellas and morehttp : **36;38356;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;38394;TOOLONG ... title= " The Strumbellas. " alt= " The Strumbellas. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;38430;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1418457 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The Strumbellas ' keyboardist , Dave Ritter , remembers watching the Canada Day festivities in Ottawa on television while growing up in Oshawa. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " If you were n't going to be out enjoying the sun , the TV was on and you watched the Parliament Hill performance , " said the 39-year-old musician , recalling Barenaked Ladies ' Canada Day appearance in ' 97 as if it happened yesterday . " It was the thing to do in my house . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Now he and his Strumbellas bandmates are among the artists performing on the Hill as part of this year 's official July 1 celebrations . Free music , performances and family activities also take place at Major 's Hill Park , the Canadian Museum of History and pretty much every other patch of green space in the Ottawa-Gatineau region over the weekend . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Of course , the focus Monday is on the Hill , where an eclectic evening lineup starts at 6:45 p.m. with a DJ set by Polaris-winning artist Kaytranada . The program also features performances by country star Brett Kissel , pop chanteuse C ? ur de Pirate , deep-voiced Indigenous singer-songwriter William Prince , Star Academy finalist Marie-Mai , country maven Crystal Shawanda , Senegalese-Canadian pop artiste Karim Ouellet , and Wavin ' Flag Somali-Canadian K'naan. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;K'naan and Ouellet appear in the daytime show , too , which begins at noon , with the sumptuous backing of the NAC Orchestra performing new arrangements of their songs under the direction of maestro Alexander Shelley . Rounding out the lineup are non-binary singer-songwriter T. Thomason and Mohawk singer Shawnee , plus the Montreal dance company , Les Ballets Jazz de Montreal , with an excerpt of their Leonard Cohen homage , Dance Me. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;For The Strumbellas , the high-profile gig is a triumphant occasion , not only a long-awaited return to the nation 's capital ( where Ritter studied English literature at Carleton University ) , but also a chance for Canadian fans to welcome them back and toast their recent international success . Although there 's only time for a few songs , they 'll be back to play a full set at CityFolk in September . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The decade-old band struck gold in 2016 with their summer hit , Spirits , an infectious anthem brimming with handclaps and a big chorus , making it a perfect crowd singalong tune . The song was a breakthrough that sent them touring around the world , earned them spots at major festivals and TV talk shows , and won the 2017 Juno for single of the year . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;After picking up that award at Canadian Tire Centre ( the Junos were in Ottawa that year ) , they turned their attention to making a follow-up album , their fourth . Rattlesnake came out this spring , and it 's decidedly more pop than folk , as Ritter noted . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " We 're always trying to find the sound that 's bigger , better , more anthemic , and this is a continuation of that , " he said . " If you plot a trajectory from our first album , it was pretty folky and has more country elements , but as the albums progress , they get poppier , with more synthesizers and big strings and things like that . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " I do n't know what happened to us , " he added . " We were fans of alt-country but we all fell in love with pop music . I think we sort of keep trying to write Taylor Swift songs on some level . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Or maybe they 're evolving -- along with the rest of the country . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Between the Toronto Raptors ' success and the legalization of cannabis , Canada is outgrowing its image as a backwoods outpost . The nation is looking hipper every day . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;On their recent travels through the United States and Europe this year , Ritter and his compadres ( singer-songwriter- guitarist &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Simon Ward , lead guitarist Jon Hembrey , violinist Isabel Ritchie , bassist Darryl James and drummer Jeremy Drury ) &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;noticed a shift in the perception of Canadians . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " Especially with Drake and Bieber , Canadian music is not a novelty anymore , " Ritter said . " It does n't feel like we 're a fish out of water . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Drake , in particular , is putting Canada on the international map . Ritter is a big hip-hop aficionado who likes to remix music , create playlists for parties and believes Drizzy should be appreciated as a cultural ambassador and game-changer. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " Anyone who 's been that consistent for that long , who 's shifted genres , personas , and modes of working but still has everything clicking all the time is just a master , " he said . " Consistency is very difficult to have as a musician , and the people who are achieving that are at the top of the game . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Still , when it comes to being Canadian , there are some stereotypes that persist . " It 's true that we 're polite and kind people , " Ritter observed . " We may joke about saying sorry when you bump into someone , but it 's a nice thing to be known around the world as a kind and generous and reasonable people . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; lsaxberg@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; **30;38460;TOOLONG DAY **34;38492;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Parliament Hill , **31;38528;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;12 p.m. to 1:45 pm&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;NAC Orchestra&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;K'naan&lt;/span &g; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Karim Ouellet&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; T. Thomason &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Shawnee&lt;/span &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Les Ballets Jazz de Montreal&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Parliament Hill , **31;38561;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;6:45 p.m. to 10 p.m. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Kaytranada ( 6:45 to 7:45 ) &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The Strumbellas&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Marie-Mai&lt;/spa ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Brett Kissel&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;C ? ur de Pirate&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Karim Ouellet&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;William Prince&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Crystal Shawanda&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;K'naan&lt;/span &g; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Major 's Hill **28;38594;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Activities run from 11 a.m. to 10 p.m. each day&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;June 30&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;CHANCES&lt;/span &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;iSKWE&lt;/span&gt; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Mehdi Cayenne&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Salebarbes&lt;/sp ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Said The Whale&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;July 1&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Elage Diouf&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Tyler Shaw &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Corneille&lt;/spa ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Tokyo Police Club&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Domeno&lt;/span &g; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;&lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Canadian Museum of **31;38624;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Tim Hortons Stage , 9 a.m. -10 p.m . July 1&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Elberlyn&lt;/span ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;River Doucette&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Silla and Rise&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Ben Caplan&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Bleu Jeans Bleu&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;DJ Shub&lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For more information , go to &lt;a href= " http : **34;38657;TOOLONG ... . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE 10/3 PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;38693;TOOLONG ... Lynn SaxbergWed , 26 Jun 2019 11:44:25 **45;38735;TOOLONG ... ArtsLocal NewsMusicThe Citizen 's music writers share their 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival pickshttp : **35;38782;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;38819;TOOLONG ... title= " Saxophonist Melissa Aldana , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Wednesday , June 26. " alt= " Saxophonist Melissa Aldana , who plays the 2019 TD Ottawa Jazz Festival on Wednesday , June 26. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;38855;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1414820 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : **34;38885;TOOLONG ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last few years , in step with the backlash against a p&amp;mdash ; &amp;ndash ; grabbing U.S. president and the rise of the MeToo Movement , it became more apparent than ever that jazz had problems with patriarchy and sexism. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Big-name jazz professors left their jobs after allegations of sexual misconduct surfaced . Prominent jazz pianists Robert Glasper and Ethan Iverson were vilified after their conversation , printed in March 2017 on Iverson&amp;rsquo ; s well-read blog , delved into a discussion of why women listeners prefer grooves over complex improvised melodies. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Women &amp;ldquo ; don&amp;rsquo ; t love a whole lot of soloing . When you hit that one groove and stay there , it&amp;rsquo ; s like a musical clitoris , &amp;rdquo ; Glasper notoriously said . &amp;ldquo ; You&amp;rsquo ; re there , you stay on that groove , and the women&amp;rsquo ; s eyes close and they start to sway , going into a trance . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Glasper&amp;rsquo ; s comments came as a shock to exactly zero people who&amp;rsquo ; ve spent any time in the jazz world , &amp;rdquo ; responded U.S. author and jazz journalist Michelle Mercer in a column that she wrote for NPR. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This scandal reveals that the issue of women in jazz goes deeper , into a gendered construction of the music itself , &amp;rdquo ; Mercer continued . &amp;ldquo ; We need an intelligent public discussion about gendered notions of jazz , and this hot mess might as well be the impetus for that discussion . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since then , the jazz world has seen more music made with a feminist slant , more all-female bands , and even the recent creation of an Institute of Jazz and Gender Justice at Boston&amp;rsquo ; s Berklee College of Music &amp;mdash ; a respected institution that had been plagued by &amp;ldquo ; a culture of blatant sexual harassment , &amp;rdquo ; according to a November 2017 Boston Globe investigation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It may well be that this year&amp;rsquo ; s TD Ottawa Jazz Festival , which starts Friday and runs until Canada Day , advances the cause of women in jazz more boldly than any of its Canadian and U.S. counterparts , thanks to its new commitment to strive for gender parity in its bookings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As usual , the festival continues to be basically two events in one &amp;mdash ; an outdoor festival based in Marion Dewar Plaza with much mass-appeal , cross-over music in the mix , plus an indoor festival based in the National Arts Centre presenting more uncompromisingly jazz-based music . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But this year , the bookings prominently featuring women &amp;mdash ; vocalists and instrumentalists alike &amp;mdash ; make up roughly half of the festival&amp;rsquo ; s bookings , from Norah Jones , Jane Siberry , Judith Hill , Cuban vocalist Omara Portuondo and former Prince guitarist Donna Grantis in the first category to the bonafide jazz stars Patricia Barber , Cyrille Aim&amp;eacute ; e , Kris Davis , Mary Halvorson , Melissa Aldana , Anna Webber and Terri Lyne Carrington , plus rising stars Nubya Garcia and Sasha Berliner . Montreal-based saxophonist and composer Christine Jensen , a two-time Juno Award winner , will lead the festival&amp;rsquo ; s first gender-balanced big band that will include Canadian jazz stars Marianne Trudel , Allison Au , Tara Davidson and Rebecca Hennessy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Ottawa may involve the best assemblage of women jazz musicians ever ! &amp;rdquo ; Mercer commented when pointed to the lineup . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other commenters lauded the festival not only for trying to equalize the presence of men and women on its stages , but simply for the excellence of the musicians , gender aside , and the lineup&amp;rsquo ; s generational shift . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While past jazz festivals in Ottawa and elsewhere could get caught up in nostalgically booking older musicians ( and attracting grey-haired listeners ) , this year&amp;rsquo ; s Ottawa festival also showcases more jazz musicians in their 30s , 40s and early 50s , including many of the women named above and also piano star Brad Mehldau , saxophonist Donny McCaslin and guitarist Gilad Hekselman. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The crush of talent seems all the more obvious given that this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival is shorter than usual , starting on a Friday rather than a Thursday and with its Monday without performances for the first time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below , music writers Peter Hum and Lynn Saxberg zero in on the concerts they&amp;rsquo ; re most excited about . Coincidentally , women figure as much as men in their picks . &lt;/p&gt; **29;38921;TOOLONG Hum 's **31;38952;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Anna Webber 's Simple Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 21 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The B.C.-born , New York-based saxophonist , flutist and composer has grown to become one of the most invigorating musicians on jazz&amp;rsquo ; s cutting edge . Be warned that Webber&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; Simple Trio&amp;rdquo ; is named archly , as her compositions are fiendishly tricky and innovative . It&amp;rsquo ; s a good thing pianist Matt Mitchell and drummer John Hollenbeck ( an American who&amp;rsquo ; s the current chair of McGill University&amp;rsquo ; s jazz department ) are world-class avant-gardists who revel in interpreting Webber&amp;rsquo ; s intriguing , challenging material . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;38985;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39021;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Gilad Hekselman Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 21 , 8 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; No less than jazz guitar hero Pat Metheny has singled out New York City-based guitarist Hekselman as a rising star . One listen to the 36-year-old native of Israel helps to explain why . Hekselman is a lucid , lyrical player at the fore of jazz&amp;rsquo ; s modern mainstream , blessed with a mastery of tone and a knack for making fresh , personal music feel accessible and even ineffably right . At the NAC&amp;rsquo ; s Fourth Stage , Hekselman will perform with Montreal-raised bassist Rick Rosato and drummer Jonathan Pinson , a supportive pair that accompanied the guitarist earlier this month in China and Japan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;39057;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39092;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Kris Davis &amp; Ingrid Laubrock&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Sunday , June 23 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2010 , Ben Ratliff , then the jazz critic for the New York Times , wrote : &amp;ldquo ; Over the last couple of years in New York , one method for deciding where to hear jazz on a given night has been to track down the pianist Kris Davis . &amp;rdquo ; In the decade since , the Calgary-raised musician has demonstrated astonishing breadth and imagination as a vital figure on jazz and creative music&amp;rsquo ; s leftmost flank . She and German saxophonist Laubrock are long-time , perfectly paired collaborators who explore bracing music that intertwines knotty compositions and unfettered improvising . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Jakob Bro / Thomas Morgan / Joey Baron&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tuesday , June 25 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the last decade , the award-winning Danish guitarist Bro , 41 , has been collaborating with the cream of the American jazz crop to make thoughtful and even dreamy music in line with ECM Records&amp;rsquo ; s evocative aesthetic . At the NAC Fourth Stage , Bro will lead a trio that includes bassist Thomas Morgan and drummer Joey Baron , two U.S. musicians with whom he has released two records on ECM in recent years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;39128;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39163;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;= Jazz Orchestra &amp; Christine Jensen&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tuesday , June 25 , 8 p.m. , NAC Studio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A large ensemble formed to wave the flag for gender parity at this year&amp;rsquo ; s festival , this group also pulls together some of the best jazz musicians of their generation in Montreal , Toronto and Ottawa . Saxophonist Christine Jensen , the two-time Juno Award winner from Montreal , Toronto-based horn players , Rebecca Hennessy , Tara Davidson and Allison Au , plus pianist Marianne Trudel will shine most brightly as this command performance will feature their compositions as well as their playing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Melissa Aldana Quartet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After winning the 2013 Thelonious Monk International Jazz Saxophone Competition at the age of 24 , the Chilean-born musician with a thrilling , virtuosic modern style saw her star rise dramatically . In Ottawa next Wednesday , Aldana kicks off a slate of especially promising concerts . Leading a quartet that includes the equally young and compelling pianist Sam Harris , Aldana will likely showcase some of the surging , heady music from her new album , Visions , whose compositions were inspired by the life and works of the late Mexican artist Frida Kahlo . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;39199;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39235;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Illegal Crowns&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 8 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The collective quartet called Illegal Crowns consists of four highly regarded and bristling improvisors : drummer Tomas Fujiwara , guitarist Mary Halvorson , cornet player Taylor Ho Bynum and pianist Benoit Delbecq . While the first three musicians , who are all New York City-based , are able to play together regularly , the quartet with French pianist Delbecq is a more sporadic project . Expect creative sparks to fly when the four strong and outward-bound personalities are reunited . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Brad Mehldau Quintet&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 8:30 p.m. , TD main stage , Marion Dewar Plaza&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There&amp;rsquo ; s seemingly little that Mehldau , the most influential pianist in jazz over the last two decades hasn&amp;rsquo ; t tried . He&amp;rsquo ; s led a breathtaking trio since the mid-1990s , dazzled as a virtuosic soloist who can delve into Bach one day and Radiohead the next , and played equally moving duets with classic soprano Renee Fleming and mandolinist Chris Thile . The 48-year-old will bring a new all-star quintet to Ottawa for what should be the most jazz-rich of the festival&amp;rsquo ; s main-stage concerts . Expect horn players Ambrose Akinmusire ( trumpet ) and Joel Frahm ( tenor saxophone ) to thrill , while drummer Leon Parker is renowned for his uniquely buoyant , minimalist swinging . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Hayden Chisholm Trio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Wednesday , June 26 , 7 p.m. , NAC Studio&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thursday , June 27 , 6 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Raised in New Zealand and based in Serbia , alto saxophonist Chisholm is an infrequent visitor to North America . That&amp;rsquo ; s a shame , because the 44-year-old is an engaging musician with a softened , appealing tone , great melodic agility and a quirky penchants for microtones and the Indian drone-producing shruti box . He will perform twice at the festival , on Wednesday with a larger ensemble that will include Ottawa-area string players , and then on Thursday with his long-time collaborators Matt Penman on bass and Jochen Rueckert on drums . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;39271;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39307;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Terri Lyne Carrington and Social Science&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thursday , June 27 , 8:30 p.m. , TD main stage , Marion Dewar Plaza&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A prodigious talent who received a full scholarship to Berklee College of Music when she was 11 , drummer Carrington has blossomed into not just a leader of formidable ensembles but also a champion of gender issues , having recently created an Institute for Gender Justice at her alma mater . In Ottawa , the 53-year-old will present her forward-thinking and politically progressive project Social Science , which includes the incandescent thirty-something musicians Aaron Parks ( keyboards ) and Matthew Stevens ( guitar ) and Kassa Overall ( DJ ) along with vocalist Debo Ray and multi-instrumentalist Morgan Guerin. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;39343;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39379;TOOLONG **28;39415;TOOLONG Saxberg 's **31;39445;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Judith Hill&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 21 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The powerhouse singer was one of the backup vocalists profiled in the documentary 20 Feet From Stardom , recognizing her work with the likes of Prince , Stevie Wonder , Elton John , Michael Jackson and more . She stepped into the spotlight with her Prince-produced 2015 debut album , which demonstrated her skill as a songwriter , too . Last year&amp;rsquo ; s follow-up , Golden Child , is another showcase of her ability to weave synth pop , gospel , rock and soul into one funky brew . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Her dad is pioneering funk bassist Robert Lee Hill ( and is in her band ) , while her mom , Michiko Hill , is an accomplished keyboardist . The couple also run a studio in L.A. Growing up , Judith always had singers in her room because her bedroom was the vocal booth . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Galactic featuring Erica Falls&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 22 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New Orleans funk-and-jazz jam band Galactic blast off in a new direction with their 10th and latest album , Already Ready Already . Produced by band members Robert Mercurio and Ben Ellman , the record finds them joining forces with an array of emerging singers , including vocalist Erica Falls , who&amp;rsquo ; s heralded as the queen of New Orleans neo-soul . She&amp;rsquo ; s also touring with the band , providing a refreshing change from the band&amp;rsquo ; s usual male-dominated groove-making. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;39478;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39514;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Galactic recently purchased Tipitina&amp;rsquo ; s , the legendary New Orleans nightclub where they&amp;rsquo ; ve been playing regular gigs for 25 years , including an annual all-nighter on the eve of Mardi Gras. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Jane Siberry&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 22 , 10:30 p.m. , OLG Stage , Confederation Park&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s free-ranging songbird returns to one of her favourite nests , Ottawa , a city where she has found inspiration and plenty of support from the folk-music community over the years . She&amp;rsquo ; ll be accompanied by a small but mighty ensemble consisting of Ottawa-born , New York-based music director Peter Kiesewalter on piano , Julian Armour on bass and Whitney LaGrange on violin . It&amp;rsquo ; s hard to predict what she&amp;rsquo ; ll play but we know that Siberry&amp;rsquo ; s arty mid-&amp;rsquo ; 80s hit , Mimi On The Beach , has been resurfacing in recent concerts , and we hear there are new tunes to unveil , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Siberry once revealed that she enjoys a simple baked potato before performing , and won&amp;rsquo ; t go on stage without lipstick and her hair done up . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **36;39550;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Sunday , June 23 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chicago has been going strong for half a century , proud of the fact that they&amp;rsquo ; ve never missed a show in 52 years . They formed in Chicago , of course , in 1967 as the Chicago Transit Authority , with the intention of writing politically charged rock &amp;lsquo ; n&amp;rsquo ; roll songs powered by a horn section . Instead they shortened the name and hit the charts with songs like If You Leave Me Now , Saturday in the Park , Hard to Say I&amp;rsquo ; m Sorry and 25 or 6 to 4 , becoming staples of the &amp;rsquo ; 70s&amp;rsquo ; wave of soft rock . Three original members are still in the band , and they still tour with several truckloads of gear , making it the biggest production of the jazz festival&amp;rsquo ; s main stage this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : In the early years of touring , the budget only allowed for two hotel rooms , one for sleeping and one for partying . Which was fine because &amp;ldquo ; we didn&amp;rsquo ; t need much sleep back in those days , &amp;rdquo ; recalls trumpeter Lee Loughnane. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; **40;39588;TOOLONG &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Thursday , June 27 , 10:30 p.m. , OLG Stage , Confederation Park&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; DakhaBrakha was created 15 years ago by an avant-garde theatre director in Kyiv to liven up his shows , but probably not to dominate them , which is what happens whenever they play . With a name that means give/take in old Ukrainian , the quartet specializes in the folk music of their homeland , reinventing it for a modern age with an unapologetic feminist message . In traditionally-inspired costumes , Marko Halanevych , Olena Tsybulska , Nina Garenetska and Iryna Kovalenko rev up the rhythms with instruments from around the world , including tabla , djembe , accordion , bass , cello , didgeridoo and more . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **33;39630;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39665;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : DakhaBrakha&amp;rsquo ; s first show at Bonnaroo in 2014 was such a hit that Rolling Stone declared them the breakout act of the festival , coining the new musical subgenre : &amp;ldquo ; Ukrainian folkdrone Bjorkpunk. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Omara Portuondo with NAC Orchestra&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 28 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The legendary Cuban singer , who&amp;rsquo ; s well into her 80s , is renowned as the diva ( and the only female voice ) of the Buena Vista Social Club . International fame came with the &amp;nbsp ; Grammy-winning Buena Vista album and an Oscar-nominated documentary , but she had been a superstar at home for decades , equally comfortable with all the Afro-Cuban styles , from boleros to rumba . On this tour , a final , worldwide journey she calls her Last Kiss tour , she&amp;rsquo ; ll be performing songs from Cuba&amp;rsquo ; s golden era with the lush and expert backing of the NAC Orchestra . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;39701;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39737;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Portuondo plays the good witch who lives in the Louisiana swamps in the Spanish version of Disney&amp;rsquo ; s animated The Princess and the Frog . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Cha Wa&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Friday , June 28 , 10:30 p.m. , OLG Stage , Confederation Park&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two of the musical traditions that make New Orleans&amp;rsquo ; street culture so rich find a common groove in Cha Wa . The New Orleans band combines the big , brassy marching band sound with Mardi Gras Indian culture to come up with a funk-laced , sousaphone-soaked twist on the party music of the Big Easy . While some of the songs have a social-justice message , you can dance to any of them . Their latest album , Spyboy , is produced by fellow jazzfest player , Ben Ellman of the band Galactic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Singer J&amp;rsquo ; Wan Boudreaux is the grandson of Big Chief Monk Boudreaux of the Golden Eagles Mardi Gras Indian tribe . For more than 100 years , Indian tribes have paraded through New Orleans on Mardi Gras , decked out in elaborate costumes they craft themselves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;The Roots&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 29 , 8:30 p.m. , TD Main Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After wowing the jazzfest crowd for the 35th anniversary edition of the festival four years ago , the legendary Philadelphia hiphop crew The Roots are back to strut more of their stuff on the main stage . Last time Jimmy Fallon&amp;rsquo ; s house band tailored their show to a jazz audience , with plenty of jazz-infused hiphop and a Herbie Hancock cover , along with crowd-pleasing surprises by the likes of Guns N Roses , Led Zeppelin and Kool and the Gang . And who knows ? Maybe they&amp;rsquo ; ll bring some of those delightful classroom instruments they use for the viral videos produced with Fallon and his superstar musical guests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : You don&amp;rsquo ; t have to go far for the after-party . Roots&amp;rsquo ; DJ Questlove plays a DJ set on the OLG Stage in Confederation Park as part of the After Dark Series . It starts at 10:30 p.m. after the main set ( June 29 ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Nubya **31;39773;TOOLONG &lt;strong&gt;Saturday , June 29 , 4 p.m. , NAC Fourth Stage&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The saxophonist is one of a generation of young musicians in London finding their way into jazz from the club scene , a wave that Rolling Stone magazine describes as a new British invasion . Raised by Caribbean parents , Garcia discovered the tenor sax at the tender age of 10 , after learning piano , violin and recorder . She made her recording debut in 2017 with the fearless 5ive EP and followed that last year with the electronic-infused When We Are . Her Afro-tinged jazz doesn&amp;rsquo ; t go wild on technique but , as an NPR reviewer said , she has an &amp;ldquo ; imploring and soulful style on tenor , with enough controlled brawn to evoke a touchstone like Sonny Rollins. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;39806;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;39842;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Fun fact : Garcia was one of the few jazz artists to showcase at SXSW in Austin last year . A month later , she was in the crowd at Coachella to see Beyonce. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; *** &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;TD Ottawa Jazz Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When : &lt;/strong&gt; June 21 to July 1 &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Where : &lt;/strong&gt; Marion Dewar Plaza , National Arts Centre , Confederation Park , Queen St. Fare , Lord Elgin Hotel , Mercury Lounge &lt;br&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Info and tickets : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;a href= " http : //ottawajazzfestival.com " &gt;ottaw ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE 10/3 PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;39878;TOOLONG ... Peter HumWed , 19 Jun 2019 11:45:02 **45;39920;TOOLONG ... ArtsMusicOn secret shows , a new record and overcoming negativity : An interview with Half Moon Runhttp : **37;39967;TOOLONG ... &lt;img src= " https : **34;40006;TOOLONG ... title= " Supplied photo of Half Moon Run . " alt= " Supplied photo of Half Moon Run . " data-portal-copyright= " Jennifer McCord " **28;40042;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1412363 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Half Moon Run spent the first five years of their career touring the world , winning over huge crowds at festivals and stadium shows with their harmony-laden , electronic-infused folk rock . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a thrill , but they also learned to value the small shows , where there&amp;rsquo ; s a more intimate connection with fans and they feel comfortable trying out new songs . In this edited interview , Devon Portielje , the Ottawa-born singer of the Montreal-based band , talks about the appeal of secret shows , what to expect on the upcoming album , due for release this fall , and how he learned to get out of a negative mindset . The band returns to Ottawa on Thursday to headline the first night of the Tim Hortons Dragon Boat Festival&amp;rsquo ; s free concert series . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : The last time I saw you guys was a secret show at a pub in Wakefield . What 's the appeal of these surprise gigs ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... Well , we usually announce them on our mailing list , and the attraction is that it 's a great show , from our perspective , but also for our fans . It 's a nice , tight , small venue . We bring in all our production , which is quite an affair now , and it 's a great place to try out new material in a safe environment . You go to a big festival and try out new material , sometimes you get a bit of a hostile audience . It 's nice to try something , and make it feel special and exclusive , perhaps . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Speaking of new material , how 's the new record coming along ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... It 's coming along great . We 're in the mixing process of it now , so it 's almost wrapped up , almost finalized . We brought up a producer , Joe Chiccarelli , who has a massive catalogue , tons of experience , tons of skills . We did about five weeks in Montreal at the studio . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;Q : &; ... &lt;strong&gt;Why did you pick Joe to produce ? **28;40072;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... His massive catalogue was definitely important , but he seemed to be able to bring music forward . I do n't know how to describe it . A little bit brighter . We wanted to kind of come forward in the speakers a little bit to be more present , shall we say . So we wanted someone who 's performance oriented , to get a good performance on tape , and bring it forward to be more present . I know he 's done artists like Elton John and Frank Zappa but you 're not so much looking at the style or the taste , I was looking for the way the audio sounds . Does it sit back in the speakers ? Is it muffled or is it present and clear ? That 's really a fine art . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Did you have the songs ready before going into the studio ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... Yes . It is somewhat of a myth to be able to walk into a studio without your songs prepared . Your producer is going to hang his head . I know Fleetwood Mac spent six weeks working on drum tones in the studio but they put a million dollars into studio rental fees . It seems to be impractical for us . A lot of the music was captured in five or six takes , and we 're all playing mostly live together so it was really quite fast , us being so rehearsed before we go into the studio . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : What 's inspiring the songwriting on this record ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... Well , I 've had kind of a negative outlook on the world for a long time , and that kind of filters into the music a little bit , but I 've been reading evolutionary psychology books , and other non-fiction stuff , and they 've changed my whole outlook . Normally I would take inspiration from pain or challenge or misery or something , but now it 's like I have maybe more of a poetic way of looking at the world . The inspiration comes from peacefulness or an observation about somebody or something different rather than the more negative thing . And also love . I met my girlfriend during the writing period . That 's an interesting process , that kind of burgeoning relationship . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;Q : Half Moon Run has had a lot of luck since the beginning . What fuelled your negative outlook on life ? &lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... I guess it 's just the media representation of facts to make it seem like there 's more crime , or more pollution , or more this than that . Humans are more geared to remember and pay attention to negative input than positive because it 's related to survival . I 'm deeply in that trap . But you know , I do n't want to get too statistical , but I guess the way it seemed before , between pollution and climate change and wealth inequality and those kinds of things , it felt like we were entering a dark phase , but I think that 's probably been a feeling of humanity since the dawn of time . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : How did you change that outlook ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... Cognitive behavioural therapy , I 've been doing that . And meditating. &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;I started the new year meditating every day , 10 minutes . I did about 80 days and then fell off a little bit . But that 's intense , controlling the mind . I guess it 's kind of biohacking . You can control your mental response to certain stimuli . It 's like I 'm using meditation as a tool . That ancient wisdom is coming back in a way that I did n't predict . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Q : Ultimately , what keeps it fun for you ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;&lt;strong&gt;A : &; ... Mindfulness in the wonderful moments . I got really used to being on the road and playing in front of 5,000 people or something during festival runs , and you just get used to those massive highs . So now , it 's like i just want to be more present . Now I enjoy the writing process so much , that moment when you 're starting a song . There are no limits , and that 's a deep challenge . A lot of artists struggle with that . When do you stop writing ? But right at that inception , that moment when there 's no limits and you can go in any direction with the song , it 's a sacred feeling . Every song we 've ever written for us has been the best song we 've ever written in our lives for a very short period of time . You have to believe in that and catch that wave to continue the spark of writing . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; lsaxberg@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Tim Horton 's Dragon Boat Festival&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;When &amp; where : &lt;/strong&gt; June 20-23 , Mooney&amp;rsquo ; s Bay Park , free admission &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;June 20&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Half Moon Run , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Jacquie Neville , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Vile Bodies &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;June 21 &lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Alan Doyle , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Jane 's Party , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Basic White &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;June 22 &lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Jess Moskaluke , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Sacha , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The Dill &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;June 23 &lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;USS , &lt;/span&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Dub ? , Trails &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;LISTEN TO THE DOWN TO BUSINESS PODCAST&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;40102;TOOLONG ...
